Every number reproduced exactly: 16, 8, 12, 20 baked and 0 from source, with the absent-method control still erroring. The measurements stand. — **BOUNDARY — WHAT THIS DEFECT IS NOT** (contributed by session verum-2b, 2026-09-04, and worth more than it looks). A SINGLETON type whose method was never written produces the IDENTICAL diagnostic. Their case: `Panic: method 'Text.append_byte' not found` — `type Text is` appears ONCE in `core/` and `append_byte` appears ZERO times; three sqlite files were calling something nobody had written. So "no method named X" splits into two causes that read the same, and the discriminator is one grep: **count the TYPE's declarations first.** More than one, with an `implement` block, is this defect; exactly one means the method is genuinely absent and the caller is wrong. Their second warning is the sharper half — `grep 'fn append_byte'` MATCHES `fn append_bytes`, so a genuinely absent method can read as present. Anchor the grep. This boundary is also why the sizing above counts only duplicated names: the singleton case is a different defect with a different owner. — **NEGATIVE RESULT THAT NARROWS THE CLAIM — STATIC METHODS ARE NOT LOST.** The 43-name census says what is at risk, not what is broken, and a probe was built to tell them apart: for every duplicated type name whose declaration AND `implement` block sit in the SAME file (so the mount path is unambiguous), call a zero-argument associated function. Twenty types qualified; eleven gave a conclusive answer and **all eleven resolve fine in the baked archive** — `Thread.builder`, `Span.noop`, `Value.null`, `TextBuilder.new`, `SpawnConfig.new`, `ListOptions.new`, `Spinner.dots`, `Arity.one`, `CpuContext.zero` among them. Zero losses. The other nine were inconclusive: the probe could not resolve them from SOURCE either, which indicts the probe, not the archive, so they are not counted either way. — **AND THE INSTANCE-ONLY EXPLANATION DID NOT SURVIVE ITS OWN TEST.** The obvious reading of that negative result — statics are structurally immune because the bake stores them behind a `$static$` marker — was put to a direct contrast rather than left as a story: 14 duplicated type names carry BOTH a zero-argument associated function and a `&self` method in the same file, so within one type, one file and one mount the ONLY difference is the kind of method. Calling both produced **zero instance-only losses**. Three types (`Arity`, `Span`, `Value`) had both kinds resolve fine; the nine that looked like losses fail from SOURCE too, so those probes are broken, not the archive; one was inconclusive. The contrast that would have shown the effect was incapable of showing it. — So the record should say what is actually established and no more. ESTABLISHED: the six files and their 67 diagnostics, baked-red and source-clean, twice measured on two independent binaries — every acceptance must rest on those. ESTABLISHED: eleven associated functions on duplicated names resolve fine in the baked archive. NOT ESTABLISHED: that the loss is confined to instance methods, or that statics are immune. My synthetic probes turned out weaker than the core-tests that first exposed the defect, which is the more useful lesson: the existing suite was the better instrument all along. — **A TRAP FOR WHOEVER ACCEPTS THE FIX: 37 `Text` METHODS NEVER REACH THE ARCHIVE.** Validating a method-resolution change needs a canary that resolves today and must still resolve after — but `method_dispatch.rs` intercepts a long list of `Text` methods at Tier 0 and answers them from Rust directly, so a canary drawn from that list is green whether the fix is right, wrong or over-broad. The intercepted names are: `can_read can_write capabilities capacity char_len contains ends_with epoch hash_value is_empty is_null is_valid len parse_bool parse_float parse_int parse_int_radix raw_ptr read remove_prefix remove_suffix starts_with strip_prefix strip_suffix to_float to_int to_lowercase to_uppercase trim trim_end trim_end_matches trim_matches trim_start trim_start_matches try_to_float try_to_int write`. Note that `starts_with` and `len` are both on it — the two most natural canaries anyone would reach for first. `ends_with_char` is NOT intercepted and is a valid choice. This is why the acceptance table must name distinguishable outcomes rather than a colour: `to_bit` appears and the canaries hold means correct; nothing moves means the fix did not take; a canary DISAPPEARING means over-broad. — **TWO MORE FROM THE ACCEPTANCE RUN (verum-2b, 2026-09-04), both found by running rather than reading.** — (i) THE ABSOLUTE COUNTS ARE BINARY-DEPENDENT — read the table above as a SHAPE, not as numbers. On a binary carrying later fixes, three of the six differ: `span/integration` 12 vs 15, `token/unit` 20 vs 50, `token/regression` 1 vs 2; the other three agree exactly. Neither reading is wrong. Only the `-> 0` is stable, so each side must capture its OWN before-number and acceptance can never be a count I wrote down here. — (ii) A TRUE POLE AND ITS CONTROL MUST NOT SHARE A COMPILATION UNIT. Their first canary put the failing call and the three canary prints in ONE program. `to_bit` fails at CHECK time, so the program never ran and the canary lines never printed — one half's failure silently erased the other half, and "no canary output" would have read as "the canary broke" rather than "the canary never got a turn". Separate files. This is the same family as the interception trap above: both produce a reading that looks like an answer and is not one. — **THIRD FACE, AND IT IS NOT A METHOD: ASSOCIATED-TYPE PROJECTION.** `core-tests/async/intrinsics/unit_test.vr` compiles clean from source and still fails against the archive with `Type mismatch: expected 'Output<ReadyFuture<Int>>', found 'Int'`. `ReadyFuture` is declared TWICE and the two are not variants of one idea: `core/async/future.vr:60` is a RECORD `{ value: Maybe<T> }`, while `core/async/select.vr:823` is an ALIAS `type ReadyFuture<T> is SelectReadyFuture<T>` whose `Future` impl carries `type Output = T`. The archive's bare slot holds one of them, so a projection written against the other does not reduce. This widens the defect beyond lost methods: ANY use of a duplicated name that depends on the descriptor's SHAPE — associated types, field access, variant lists (the original `AeadKind` symptom) — reads the wrong type. Methods were simply the loudest symptom. — **SCOPE OF THE WRITTEN FIX — IT COVERS ONE LEG OF TWO.** (Branch `fix/bake-dupname-method-keys` was DELETED 2026-09-04 after it was found to break 125 files — see the regression paragraph below. This paragraph is kept because it is what I believed at the time, and the gap between it and the outcome is the point.) It changes the function-descriptor key and the loader's probe order, so it addresses the METHOD leg. It does NOT touch `archive_metadata.rs:~944`, where the bare TYPE slot is still last-wins, and that is the leg behind the associated-type mismatch and the `AeadKind` variant-list symptom. So a partial result on the six-file table is the expected outcome, not a failed patch: `Capability.to_bit` reappearing is the fix working, while a file left red on a shape-dependent diagnostic is the second leg still open. Which of the six move and which do not is worth more than a verdict — nothing has yet measured how the 67 diagnostics divide between the two legs. — **THE FIX WAS BUILT, BAKED AND MEASURED — IT IS INERT, AND THE REASON REFUTES THE "TWO LEGS" FRAMING.** verum-2b cherry-picked it and confirmed the bake actually re-ran (binary v34 at 14:01; `runtime.vbca` 12:59 -> 13:51, 25009877 -> 25010896 bytes; precompile-bootstrap rebuilt 13:35, AFTER the 13:27 cherry-pick). No movement: `to_bit` still absent, `capability/unit_test.vr` 16 -> 16, canaries unchanged. — The +1019 bytes proved keys WERE written, so they read them out of `runtime.core_metadata`: — Capability.to_bit                           x4   the old bare slot core.architecture.types.Capability.to_bit   x1   WRONG TYPE core.mem.Capability.to_bit                  x1   WRONG GRANULARITY core.mem.capability.Capability.to_bit       x0   what the loader probes — (a) GRANULARITY. `to_bit` lives in module `core.mem.capability`; the bake wrote the PARENT, `core.mem`, while the loader probes the full path. The two halves are each reasonable and never meet — the bake-spelling versus load-key drift class. — (b) THE HALF THAT SETTLES IT. `core.architecture.types.Capability` does not declare `to_bit` at all, and it received a qualified key for it. So `method_keys` is built from a parent-type resolution that had ALREADY chosen the wrong `Capability` by the bare-slot rule, and the patch faithfully propagates that mis-association into the qualified namespace. **Qualifying a key cannot help when the qualification itself comes from the wrong type.** Repairing the granularity alone would yield a correctly-spelled key attached to a type without the method. — So the type leg is NOT a separable second half — it is UPSTREAM of the method leg, and the ordering asserted above is wrong. The mechanism reading survives: the bake really was aliasing under a bare first-wins key and the loser really did vanish. What does not survive is the claim that the method leg could be fixed downstream of it. `Capability` is declared FOUR times, not two — `database/common/capability.vr:173` (a protocol), `logic/separation.vr:259`, `architecture/types.vr:232`, `mem/capability.vr:96`. **THE PATCH WAS NOT HARMLESS — IT BROKE 125 FILES, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** "Additive and harmless, it simply does not reach" was written here on the strength of one acceptance table: `to_bit` unchanged, three canaries unchanged. Both true, and both about the SIX files the table covered. — verum-2b bisected four binaries: v32 (13:06) clean, v33 (13:25) clean, **v34 (14:01) shows `error<E402>: module `core.base.Int64` not found`**, v35 the same. The only Rust change in that window is this patch, cherry-picked at 13:27. The revert (`2162b6909`) landed at 14:45 — three minutes AFTER v35 finished building, so every measurement taken with v34 or v35 carries it. — DAMAGE: 127 files acquired that E402 and **125 fail on nothing else**, so a census reporting 460 failures over 2561 should have read 335. The patch did not merely fail to help — it became the largest single entry in the backlog it was written to reduce. — THE LIKELY MECHANISM, and it is the third distinct way this repair was wrong: the bake half writes keys of the form `<module>.<Type>.<method>` into `meta.functions`. Those are DOTTED NAMES that look exactly like module paths, and something in resolution reads a prefix of one — `core.base.Int64` — as a module. Qualifying a key did not just fail to fix the wrong resolution (recorded above); it manufactured names that a different resolver mistakes for its own. — **CONFIRMED AT THE ARTEFACT, AND THE PREDICATE IS NARROWER THAN THE NAMESPACE.** The v35 metadata carries `core.base.Int64.BITS`, `.MAX`, `.abs`, `.abs_diff` — **109 keys under that one prefix, 55,787 `module.Type.method` keys in total, 136 distinct `core.base.<Type>` prefixes**. The namespace was manufactured exactly as guessed. — But the namespace alone does NOT break resolution, which is the useful half. `core.base.Bool` exists too (28 keys) and mounts clean. Testing the prefixes one at a time: `Bool`, `Text`, `Byte`, `Char`, `Int32`, `UInt64`, `Cell`, `Int`, `Float` all CLEAN; **only `Int64` and `Float64` give E402** — precisely the two names that have a canonical/surface twin (`ty.rs:2660` `primitive_named("Int64")`, `:2695` `Float64`, with `"i64"\|"I64" => "Int64"` in the normalisation table). `Int32` has no such entry and carries 110 keys, MORE than Int64's 109, so it is not a threshold on key count. — THE PREDICATE, then: **a name that is one of a canonical/surface pair, mounted by its non-surface spelling, with the manufactured keys present.** Two conditions, both necessary. That places the defect where the two-name pair is reconciled, not where primitives are enumerated — a much cheaper thing to find. And it is the third and sharpest way the patch was wrong: it did not fail to repair a resolution, it taught a DIFFERENT resolver a false fact, and only for names that already had two truths to reconcile. — WHAT I OWE THE NEXT READER: the branch was labelled UNVERIFIED and the register said not to land it, and it was landed to measure it. That sequence was reasonable and the outcome is still mine — an acceptance table covering six files was read as covering the tree, which is the same error as a fixed-stride sampler read as covering the tree, two rows up. — **A REBAKE TRAP THAT TURNED OUT NOT TO APPLY — recorded because the RETRACTION is the useful part.** `build.rs:1676` composes the stdlib cache key as `blake3(SCHEMA_VERSION \|\| core_files)`, which reads as though a change to the bake's own logic could not invalidate it: the build would reuse the archive produced by the OLD code, every acceptance file would read as before, and "no change" would be indistinguishable from "the fix never ran". That was raised, and then refuted by grepping rather than by reading further prose: `crates/verum_compiler/src/archive_metadata.rs` is `build.rs:1978`, one of 23 entries in the curated `codegen_paths` array (declared :1927, mixed in at :2090). The `:1676` expression is the SHAPE of the key, not the list of its terms. Editing that file invalidates the key on its own; no manual rebake is needed. The other patched file, `verum_types/src/infer/core.rs`, is correctly absent — it is the loader side, which runs at check time and cannot affect what the bake writes. — Two things survive, and they are why this stays in the row. First, the general trap is real and HAS burned this repo: the VBC wire files were genuinely missing from that list and four stale compilers shipped — so `codegen_paths` being curated rather than derived is a standing hazard, and the check is one grep. Second, and independent of whether the cache-key story is right: **confirm the bake actually re-ran before reading any number** — the build log's `Embedded stdlib: N files, …KB compressed` line, and `runtime.vbca` newer than the patch. That check costs nothing and does not depend on the argument being correct, which is the reason to prefer it to the argument. — **A CONFOUND I RAN TWENTY MEASUREMENTS BEFORE CONTROLLING FOR: THE INSTRUMENT'S AGE.** "Baked-red and source-clean" has a second explanation that has nothing to do with the archive — a compiler built on 2026-08-31 carries an archive from 2026-08-31, while a source-driven run reads `core/` as it is TODAY. Between those dates `core/` took **48 commits across 269 files**. So a test that someone repaired last week reads exactly like a bake defect, and the whole method is confounded unless the baked side is measured on a CURRENT binary. — Re-measured on a binary built the same day, with `mem/capability/unit_test.vr` as the control: — mem/capability/unit_test.vr    aug31 17  ->  sep04 17    genuine meta/contexts/unit_test.vr     aug31 20  ->  sep04 16    genuine, partly repaired since meta/attribute/unit_test.vr    aug31  3  ->  sep04  3    genuine math/autodiff/unit_test.vr     aug31 29  ->  sep04  0    STALE — not a defect at all logic/separation/unit_test.vr  aug31  5  ->  sep04  0    STALE — not a defect at all — Two of five were the calendar, not the archive. The six original files survive this because their baked side was independently reproduced on a fresh binary by another session before any of them was recorded — which is the only reason the headline stands. The lesson generalises past this row: **the age of the compiler is an input to every baked-vs-source comparison**, and a stale binary reports the tree's PAST as the archive's fault. — **RE-MEASURED ON A NAMED, IMMUTABLE SNAPSHOT — and the instrument is now part of the record.** A peer's live target directory is a MOVING target (v32, v33, v34 all pass through the same path), so "measured on their binary" states nothing. Everything below was measured on `verum_v32`, sha256 `1452fdec87f1d17e`, built 13:06 on 2026-09-04, copied out and never rewritten. Error-line counts, which include the trailing `compilation failed with N` line: — mem/capability/unit_test.vr        17 -> 0 mem/capability/regression_test.vr   9 -> 0 meta/span/integration_test.vr      16 -> 0 meta/token/unit_test.vr            51 -> 0 meta/token/property_test.vr        11 -> 0 meta/token/regression_test.vr       3 -> 0 meta/contexts/unit_test.vr         16 -> 0   (new) meta/contexts/property_test.vr     18 -> 0   (new) meta/attribute/unit_test.vr         3 -> 0   (new) — NINE files. `math/autodiff` and `logic/separation` read 0 on this snapshot too, confirming they were the calendar and not the archive. The absent-method control still fires under the source-driven stdlib on this binary, so the zeros are resolutions that succeeded rather than checks that were skipped. — **A THIRD CONFOUND, THIS ONE SELF-INFLICTED, CAUGHT AND CLEARED.** The source side of every reading above was taken against a COPY of `core/`, and that copy still carried an abandoned experiment of mine — `AeadKind` renamed to `QuicAeadKind` across four QUIC files, made hours earlier while testing whether the collision was causal. So the "clean from source" verdicts were, for a while, partly a verdict on my own edit; `net/tls13` in particular could have been clean BECAUSE I had removed the duplicate. Found by diffing the copy against `core/` — 8 files differed, 4 of them mine. — Re-measured against a pristine copy (`diff -rq` = 0 files) on the same `verum_v32` snapshot, control still firing: `net/tls13/property_test.vr`, `mem/capability/unit_test.vr`, `meta/token/unit_test.vr`, `meta/contexts/unit_test.vr` and `meta/attribute/unit_test.vr` all read **0 from source on both copies**. The rename flattered nothing and the table stands. The general rule, which cost nothing to apply and would have cost the whole day to miss: **a working copy used as a control must be diffed against its original before its answers are believed** — an experiment left in the corpus is indistinguishable from a property of the corpus. — **THE CLEAREST INSTANCE OF LEG TWO YET — RECORD versus NEWTYPE.** `core-tests/sys/mod/unit_test.vr` is source-clean on `verum_v32` and the archive still answers `cannot index type 'Fd' — only tuple types support .0`. `Fd` is declared twice and the two shapes could not be further apart: — core/shell/resources.vr:56   public type Fd is { … }      RECORD core/sys/io_engine.vr:203    public type Fd is (Int32);   NEWTYPE — `.0` is valid on the newtype and meaningless on the record; the archive's bare slot holds the record, so a program written against the newtype cannot index it. No method is involved, and no associated type either — this is plain FIELD ACCESS, the third distinct symptom after method lookup and associated-type projection. It is also the easiest instance to explain to someone who does not believe the mechanism: two declarations, one name, opposite shapes, and the archive keeps whichever it saw last. — **FULL COUNT ON A CONTROLLED INSTRUMENT: 31 FILES, 203 DIAGNOSTICS.** Candidates were the 175 of 1263 `core-tests` files that mention any of the 68 duplicated names carrying an `implement` block; each was then measured on snapshot `verum_v32` (sha256 `1452fdec87f1d17e`) against a PRISTINE copy of `core/`, with the absent-method control firing throughout. **31 files are baked-red and source-clean**, carrying 203 diagnostics — largest first: `meta/token/unit` 50, `meta/contexts/property` 17, `mem/capability/unit` 16, `meta/span/integration` 15, `meta/contexts/unit` 15, `sys/init/unit` 11, `meta/quote/unit` 11. FOUR candidates were phantoms of the stale binary (`base/ops/unit`, `base/protocols/try_protocol_agnostic`, `logic/separation/unit`, `math/autodiff/unit`) and TWO carry real errors from source. — That 203 is a LOWER BOUND twice over: the candidate filter only admits files that NAME an at-risk type, and the candidate pass itself ran on the stale binary, so any file green there and red on `verum_v32` never reached the confirmation. The honest claim is 203 measured, not 203 total. — **THE SIZING NUMBERS WERE MINE AND THEY WERE WRONG — CORRECTED TO 132 / 280 / 68.** My census regex required `is` to follow the type name directly, which is blind to EVERY generic declaration: `type ReadyFuture<T> is` has `<T>` in between. `ReadyFuture` — a type already written into this row as a worked instance of leg two — counted as ZERO declarations in my own census. The correct figures come from `scripts/ci/check_type_name_collisions.py`, whose `declares_a_type` is written against the grammar and carries `type Tree<T> is …` in its self-test: **132 colliding names over 280 declarations, 68 of them carrying an `implement` block over 144 declarations.** Every earlier figure here (113 / 241 / 43 / 93) was an undercount and has been replaced; the second ratchet added to that gate already used the gate's own count and is unaffected. — Worth stating rather than quietly fixing: this row spends several paragraphs warning that a census answers about what it KEYS ON, and then reported one keyed on a pattern that excluded generics. It was caught by another session's classifier, built for a different purpose, whose own self-test failed on the same blind spot first. The rule that would have caught it unaided is already written above — put a KNOWN carrier through the census as a control. `ReadyFuture` was sitting in the text three paragraphs up. — **A SINGLE-DECLARATION TYPE CAN STILL LOSE ITS NAME — the expansion is not the collision.** `core/tracing/tracer.vr:342` binds `List.with_capacity(...)` and reports `List<PhNoop(Unit) \| PhSimple(...)>` — the variant list where `ProcessorHandle` should be. That type is declared ONCE (`core/tracing/data.vr:1209`), and annotating the binding `List<ProcessorHandle>` changes nothing: 2 errors before, 2 after, same expanded type. So this is the row's ORIGINAL symptom in its pure form — `Named` expanded to `Variant` with the name discarded — isolated from the duplicate-name mechanism that produces the other four faces. Worth having as a clean instance: it shows the expansion is upstream of the collision rather than caused by it. — **A CHEAP TEST FOR WHETHER AN E404 IS THIS ROW OR A MISSING ANNOTATION.** Annotating the binding normally CONSTRAINS the type. Where this defect is in play it ACCUMULATES instead: `core/sys/no_runtime.vr:80` binds `Full(v) => { let result = v; … }` and reports `_`; annotate `: T` and it reports `&_`; annotate `: &T` and it reports `&&_`. Each annotation adds a reference level rather than settling one, which is the by-reference match binding this row is about, seen from the author's side. So: annotate once. If the inferred type gains a `&`, stop — the file is blocked here and no spelling will fix it. If it resolves, it was simply an unannotated binding. That distinction costs one compile and separates the 47 E404-only files into work and non-work. In practice the accumulation is not always one level: `core/collections/multiset.vr` reports `Multiset<&&&&&&&_>` — SEVEN — which is what the discriminator looks like when the value has passed through several by-reference bindings before the annotation is attempted. Seven `&` is also the most legible statement of the defect anyone has produced: the type is not being narrowed at all, it is being wrapped once per hop. — **AND THE TEST IS CHEAPER STILL — READ THE TYPE, DO NOT EDIT.** In four `collections` files the `&` is already present in the FIRST diagnostic, before any annotation is attempted: `set.vr` reports `List<&_>` for a binding ALREADY written `let mut result: List<T>`, `btree.vr` reports `&_`, `adjacency_list.vr` reports `List<Edge<&_, _>>`. `set.vr` is the conclusive one — the annotation this row's remedy would add is already there and is being ignored. So the rule is: **if the inferred type in the error already contains `&`, the file is A55-blocked and no edit is warranted.** Only when it is a bare `_` is an annotation worth trying. `deque.vr` looked like that exception — `[_]`, no `&` — and annotating its six `make_contiguous()` bindings with the declared `&mut [T]` took it from 1 error to 54, reporting `&mut [&&_]` and `&mut [&&&_]`. So a bare `_` is necessary and not sufficient; the annotation attempt remains the second half of the test, and it must be reverted on any growth in `&`. — **THE SPLIT, MEASURED READ-ONLY OVER ALL 47.** Applying the cheap half of the test to every E404-only file, with a healthy `core/` file as control: — 14  already clean   (repaired today — see the fix commits) 8  A55-blocked     (`&` present in the FIRST diagnostic) 25  worth trying    (bare `_`, or a `_` nested in a concrete shell) — 25 is an UPPER bound and known to be one: `collections/deque.vr` sits in it reporting a clean `[_]`, and annotating it took the file from 1 error to 54. So the second half of the test — annotate, then revert on any growth in `&` — still has to run per file. What the read-only pass buys is skipping eight files outright and bounding the mechanical work at 25 rather than 47. — RE-MEASURED after the day's repairs, same pass, same control: **24 clean / 8 A55-blocked / 15 worth trying**. Ten of the 25 were cleared, the A55 count did not move (as it should not — those files were never work), and the remaining 15 is what is actually left of this class. Between the two runs the split is also a check on the classification itself: a file that moved from "worth trying" to "clean" confirms the read-only test admitted it correctly, and none moved INTO the A55 bucket. — **AND THE DEFECT REACHES `core/` ITSELF, AS "FIELD NOT FOUND".** Two files in the known-failures backlog fail with a field error whose cause is this row, not a rename: — walker.vr          field 'kind' not found on 'Finding' available: [message, severity, where_] `integrity_walker_api/finding.vr:71` declares Finding{kind, detail} `cli/verify.vr:43`                  declares Finding{severity, where_, message} access_planning.vr field 'primary_key' not found on 'ColumnMeta' `l6_session/schema_cache.vr:44` and `mysql/binlog_rows.vr:142` — `walker.vr` MOUNTS the first `Finding` explicitly and is answered with the second one's fields. Two declarations with disjoint field sets, one bare name. So "field not found" joins method lookup, associated-type projection and variant matching as a face of the same collision — and it is the most misleading of them, because it names a field and invites a rename that would be wrong. — THE DISCRIMINATOR, since the two are indistinguishable from the message: **count the TYPE's declarations.** More than one means this row. Exactly one means a genuine rename and the caller should be fixed. Both were live today: `TbsCertificate` is declared ONCE, so `serial_number_bytes` really had been renamed to `serial_number` and three call sites across `crl_client.vr` and `ocsp_client.vr` were stale — repaired. `Finding` and `ColumnMeta` are declared twice and must be left alone. — **HOW MANY COLLISIONS CAN MISREPORT A FIELD: 38.** Of the duplicated names, 44 are RECORD types, and **38 of those have field sets that differ between their declarations** — the precondition for the diagnostic above. Five have NOTHING in common at all: `CpuContext`, `ExceptionFrame`, `Notification`, `ColumnMeta`, `ListOptions`, each two declarations sharing zero fields. `Finding` and `ColumnMeta` both land in the set, which is the census's control. The six that agree on their fields are the harmless ones — a collision between identical shapes reports nothing, which is exactly why the count of collisions (132) has never predicted the damage. — **THE BACKLOG'S FIELD ERRORS, CLASSIFIED: 17 ARE THIS ROW, 12 ARE REAL RENAMES.** Every `field 'x' not found on type 'T'` across the 303-file backlog, split by counting T's declarations: — A55, do not touch     17 hits over  7 types  (ParseError, ColumnMeta, Finding, ListOptions, …) genuine rename        12 hits over  6 types  (App, PageHeader, Certificate, BufferCursor, JwtPayload, BasicConstraints) — The first run of that classification reported the REVERSE — 28 renames, 1 collision — because it extracted the type name with `tr -d "type '"`, which deletes those CHARACTERS rather than that string: `ColumnMeta` became `ColumnMa` and matched no declaration, so nearly everything looked like a singleton. It was caught only because the output contradicted three types already checked by hand. Three controls now run first (`ParseError`=2, `ColumnMeta`=2, `TbsCertificate`=1) and the split above is the corrected pass. Trusting the first output would have produced a 28-item worklist of which 17 were instructions to compile code against the wrong type. — **AND "GENUINE RENAME" IS NOT THE SAME AS "MECHANICAL".** Of the six rename types, `BasicConstraints.ca` -> `.is_ca` was a one-line change and `Certificate.tbs` needed the file's own parse-and-map idiom (`Certificate` holds DER only; the parsed view carries `tbs`) — both done. But `JwtPayload` is not a rename at all once you look: `security/jwt.vr:320` declares `{header, claims: JsonValue, signing_input}` while `security/oidc.vr` reads `aud`, `exp`, `iss`, `iat`, `nbf` and `custom` at seven sites. The flat claim fields were folded INTO a JSON value, there are no claim accessors on `JwtPayload`, and repairing it means writing JSON extraction inside OIDC token validation. That is a design change in security-critical code, not a rename, and it is left alone deliberately — the classification says a field moved, never that moving it back is safe. — **A FIFTH SHAPE, AND IT IS NOT A COLLISION EITHER: A TYPE WHOSE FIELDS ARE MISSING BECAUSE ITS OWN MODULE DOES NOT COMPILE.** `core/cli/repl.vr` reports `field 'spec' not found on type 'App'` with `Available members: [arg, find_at_path, from_spec, head, register, run_or_exit, try_run, with_help_style]` — every METHOD and no FIELD. `App` is declared ONCE (`cli/runtime.vr:146`) and does have `spec`. What it also has is a module that fails to compile: `cli/runtime.vr` is itself in the known-failures list, blocked by the duplicated `ParseError`. So the descriptor consumers see carries the impl block and not the record body. The consumer is not broken and must not be edited; it is downstream of another file's failure, and repairing the upstream file is the only thing that can clear it. Add that to the declaration-count triage: **one declaration, fields absent but methods present, and the declaring module in the failures list** means look upstream. — **ONE COLLISION WORTH RESOLVING BY HAND, WITH ITS RADIUS MEASURED.** `ParseError` blocks two files at once — `cli/runtime.vr` directly, and `cli/repl.vr` downstream of it through the degraded `App` descriptor. The two declarations are not variants of one idea: — core/cli/error.vr:202        { kind: ParseErrorKind, field, value, … }   rich, CLI-specific core/base/protocols.vr:1544  { message: Text }                            generic, with `new(msg)` — Radius: 13 files under `core/cli/` name `ParseError`, 11 elsewhere. Renaming the CLI one to `CliParseError` is the natural direction — it is the domain-specific of the two and the generic one sits in `base/protocols` where a bare name belongs — but it is a PUBLIC stdlib type, so the rename is an API change and 13 files move with it. Not attempted here because it cannot be validated without a rebake, and a half-applied rename across 13 files is worse than the collision. Recorded so that whoever has a bake window can take it: this single rename clears two backlog files and removes one of the 38 field-misreporting collisions. — **THE LESSON ABOVE ALL THE OTHERS IN THIS ROW — A RECORDED RULE IS NOT A GUARD.** Two sessions worked this row today and each made an error that their OWN written rule already forbade. Mine: "put a known carrier through the census as a control" is stated in this row, I had `ReadyFuture` in hand as that carrier, and I did not run it — so a census blind to generics went into the register as sizing. Theirs: "grep for the live process before reaping" was in their notes from the previous time they broke it; instead they invented a fresh guard from cargo's build-script mtime, which pointed at the wrong unit-hash directory while a bake ran at 70% CPU under another, and they edited `core/` mid-bake. Neither error was a gap in knowledge. Both were rules that existed, were correct, were relevant, and were not executed. The remedy is not to write the rule better — it is to make running it part of the instrument, which is why the collision gate now ships its extractor's self-test and why their classifier refuses to classify when a control fails. — **A CAVEAT THE 31-FILE NUMBER HAS TO CARRY, discovered by A56.** The source-driven stdlib is a SECOND OPINION, not a gold standard, and the two paths disagree in BOTH directions. Here the baked path is the strict one — 31 files it rejects and source accepts. In A56 the SOURCE path is the strict one — it rejects a prelude-provided `Clone` impl that the archive accepts. So "source-clean" cannot mean "the test is correct" by itself; it means the other path accepted it, and the other path is demonstrably wrong about at least one thing. — What still holds the 31 up: the absent-method control fires under source throughout, so checking is live rather than skipped; and the diagnostics are of kinds (`no method named`, `cannot index`, `field not found`) whose stdlib-side cause has been traced to a bare-name key in `archive_metadata.rs`, not inferred from the disagreement alone. The measurement that would settle it outright is the fix itself: files that move when the method key is repaired were leg one, and files that do not are leg two or something else. Until then the number is 31 files where two paths disagree in a direction a located mechanism explains — which is weaker than "31 broken files" and stronger than a bare count. \| P2 | — | `crates/verum_types/src/infer/modules.rs:16115,16335`; `crates/verum_types/src/infer/patterns.rs:788,1196`; `core-tests/sys/signal/property_test.vr:171`; `core/sys/signal.vr:152` | the same code binds the same way whether the enum is local or mounted |
| A-COG | ~~**A stdlib free function mounted by a NON-ENTRY cog module never resolves.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-04 (T1126)**. `src/reader.vr` mounts `core.encoding.json` and calls `stringify`; unless `src/main.vr` ALSO mounts it, the call compiles to a stub and dies at run time (`[lenient] stage-5 … stub never resolved`) while `verum check` reports zero errors. Builtin-type METHODS from the same module work, which is why most cog code never meets it. Carrier: the name harvester feeding `wanted` matched five `ItemKind` variants and ended in `_ => {}`, swallowing `ItemKind::Module`. | P0 | T1126 | a cog whose sibling module mounts a stdlib module the entry file never names runs correctly; spec `vcs/specs/L2-standard/modules/stdlib_mount_in_sibling_resolves.vr` |
| A-SLICE | **`&record.field` coerces to an EMPTY slice for a slice-taking stdlib function.** `Text.from_utf8(&r.body)` on a 10-byte `List<Byte>` field returns `Ok("")`; `&r.body[..]`, a clone, and a user function taking `&List<Byte>` all read it correctly. Returns `Ok`, not `Err` — the caller gets a well-formed answer about no data. Affects every stdlib function with a `&[T]` parameter, and a struct field is the ordinary place to keep bytes. | P0 | T1130 | the boundary table in the task reproduces all-correct; a spec covering field / let-bound ref / local / param / clone |
| A-META | **An undeclared `@name(...)` compiles to `nil` with no diagnostic.** `@zzznotathing(1)` type-checks and prints `nil`; `@abs(1,2,3)` silently discards the extra arguments; `@sqrt("text")` yields `nil`; `@abs()` surfaces as an *internal compiler error*. The codegen implements twenty `@`-builtins and ends in a `_ =>` wildcard emitting `LoadNil`, whose comment claims a warning that is not emitted. Two L0-critical specs use `@max` / `@sqrt`; both are already red for unrelated type errors, so nothing is silently passing through a `nil` — but `001_creation.vr`'s `expected 'Bool', found 'Unit'` IS this defect surfacing at the point of use rather than at the undeclared name. | P0 | T1124 | an `@name(...)` in neither the grammar's 21 `meta_function_name`s nor a declared macro is refused by name; arity and argument types checked; `@abs()` becomes a user diagnostic |
| A-CTXW | **Context validation reports every context as undeclared AND as unused.** 31 warnings over 12 functions in one cog: `Context 'Clock' has no matching context declaration` fires even though `public context Clock` is declared in a sibling module (same blind spot as A-COG), and `declared in 'using' clause but never used` fires for contexts required by a CALLEE — which the compiler separately REQUIRES via `E613`. Measured symptom: `let r: &Int = { let v = 5; &v }; print(*r);` compiles and runs, caught only by the runtime generation guard. (2) The converter's only test is `diagnostics.rs:852 test_empty_analysis_result`, which feeds `TierAnalysisResult::empty()` and asserts the output is empty — it would pass against a body of `List::new()`. | A-ARITY | **A type NAME can carry fewer arguments than the type has parameters, and every consumer downstream reads that list BY INDEX.** Measured 2026-09-07 with a verdict trace on the receiver-instantiation leg: `impl_k` (how many type parameters the type HAS) printed beside `args.len()` (how many the receiver record CARRIES) — `ListIter<T>` 1 against 0, `MappedIter<ListIter, F>` 2 against 1. Short by exactly the count of arguments that are bare type parameters, which fail to resolve BY DESIGN. **Three producers built `TypeRef::Instantiated { args }` with `filter_map`**, so an unresolvable argument VANISHED and everything after it shifted down a slot: `type_name_to_type_ref_mono` (from a NAME), `resolve_type_ref_scoped` (from the AST — with a comment claiming "positional recovery happens at the callsite witness layer", which a drop is what destroys), and constructor-return naming, where `filter_map` PLUS `!is_empty()` accepted a PARTIAL list and rendered `Pair<Int>` for a two-parameter type — wrong arity stated confidently, then parsed back by the first producer. **The consumer proves the intent**: `for (i, ra) in rargs.iter().take(impl_k).enumerate()` is positional AND already skips `TypeRef::Generic(_)`, so it was written expecting placeholders that the producers deleted. **And the rule was already written fifteen lines above the first site** — the tuple branch of the same function says "any unparseable element fails the WHOLE tuple (arity honesty — same rule as the descriptor-string parser)". One branch kept it; its sibling did not. **FIXED at all three (144c13129)** via one helper that substitutes `Generic(TypeParamId(i))` at the argument's own index; measured `1 = 1` and `2 = 2` with the four gauge suites unmoved, and the pole reports VACUOUS rather than a clean zero when it reads no rows. **THE REMAINING HALF IS A NAME THAT CARRIES NO ARGUMENTS AT ALL**: a range expression types as bare `"Range"`, so it never enters the branch fixed here, and `Range.collect` keeps its element slot `Generic` — Tier 1 aborts on `from_iter`. Traced to one link: both name inferrers answer `None` for an UNSUFFIXED integer literal (deliberately — its type is context-dependent), and `literal_default_type_name`, which the consuming leg already calls for its receiver, was not consulted one link earlier. | P1 | T1228 (arity, fixed), T1214 (the bare name); `vcs/specs/L0-critical/vbc/collect-return-param-vs-variant-name.vr` | that spec prints `range=5 mapped=5 abi=Stdcall second=C` at BOTH tiers — today Tier 1 aborts before the first line, and the two negative controls are the half a name-guessing fix would break |
| A-TYPARAM | **One rule, eleven copies, two thresholds that disagree by CRATE.** The compiler decides whether a `Named` type is an unresolved GENERIC PARAMETER by how the name is SPELT — short and uppercase — rather than by consulting a binding. Measured: 11 sites. `verum_smt` + `verum_types` use `name.len() == 1` (5); `verum_vbc/codegen` uses `name.len() <= 2` (6). The split is exactly by crate, so a type named `Io`, `Ex` or `Ok` is a type PARAMETER to the bytecode codegen and a CONCRETE TYPE to the unifier — each copy locally consistent, so no test can see the divergence. `unify.rs:1801` records that six FURTHER copies were ruled out by instrumentation before the right one was found, and states its own trade honestly (the length test replaced a hardcoded `"Ok" \| "No" \| "Eq" \| "Fn" \| "IO"` exclusion list). **DO NOT "TIGHTEN" A COPY:** T1206 measured `Result<Int, Foo>` misreading a `Text` payload under AOT while `Result<Int, F>` reads it correctly — thirteen names, clean break at length 2 — so the single-letter branch is the one that ANSWERS CORRECTLY and the ordinary concrete path is the broken one. Making the heuristic stricter routes the last working case into the bug. The class: the compiler asks the SPELLING of an identifier a question only the BINDING can answer, and each place that asks invents its own threshold. Siblings: A-NAMEOWN (a name owning a slot), T1191 (a name deciding a binding). | P1 | ratchet `make check-type-param-name-rule`; `unify.rs:1801`, `dependent_match.rs:1062`, `vbc/codegen/expressions.rs:26027` | one predicate with one threshold, consulted from a binding; ratchet count falls from 11 and the gate refuses a stale baseline in both directions |
| A-UNTYPED | **A value that lost its static type prints as its POINTER, and nothing objects.** Tier 0 self-describes (NaN-boxed), so the interpreter is right whatever the codegen knew; Tier 1 decides at the format site from a per-register MARK, and every path that fails to stamp one produces the same output: a ten-digit decimal that changes between runs. Three sites measured 2026-09-06, three different causes, one symptom — **T1206** (`GetVariantData` does not classify a payload extracted from a user sum nested inside a `Result`; `docs/by-example/04-result-error` shipped printing an address for at least three binaries, rc=0), **T1207** (a `Text` field read through a type parameter — `generic_type_args` is consulted by the reader and set by only two producers in `vbc_lowering.rs`), **T1210** (`lower_ctx_get` stamps NOTHING on its destination, so a `CtxGet` receiver has no type, `CallM` cannot resolve the method, and `resolved_return_type` is None). They converge at `ToString` BY CONSTRUCTION: an unmarked `Text` and an `Int` are the same i64 there, which is why the symptom is shared and merging the causes was wrong all three times. **WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT:** the programs exit 0 and print a plausible number, so `check-examples-run` (does it RUN) and every rc-based gate pass. The one instrument that saw it was the by-example blast radius, which reported `MOVED` on those chapters between binaries that could not reach them — read as noise for three sessions, because an ASLR address IS noise in a comparison and a defect in the program at the same time. The harness now separates them: each program is run TWICE on ONE binary and a self-disagreeing one reports `NONDET`, not `MOVED`. Measured denominator: of 22 shipped examples, exactly 2 were nondeterministic and both were this. **T1207 IS NARROWED AND ITS TYPE IS RECOVERABLE**, measured 2026-09-07 by the row's owner: two substituted `Text` fields print as two DIFFERENT addresses 192 bytes apart — so the reads are correct and each field comes from its own slot — and `.len()` on those same values answers 4 at BOTH tiers, so the values ARE real `Text`. **That last inference was over-read and is retracted by the bytecode:** `.len()` lowers to the `Len` OPCODE, which handles its operand dynamically — it works without any type being resolved, so it is evidence about the VALUE and not about dispatch. The type is missing at ONE place, the format substitution. **LOCATED 2026-09-07:** `lower_field_named`'s merge block stores the phi as a bare i64 and stamps NOTHING on the destination — neither `mark_register_from_return_type` nor `set_obj_register_type` — so the following `ToString` has nothing to dispatch on. `Int` is indistinguishable from correct there, which is why Int controls could not see it. The information is already in scope: the candidate list is built by scanning `td.fields` BY NAME, so the field descriptor and its type are in hand, and the stamp is sound whenever the candidates AGREE — stamping one of several disagreeing types would reinstate FIELD-ACCESS-BYNAME-1's guess in a new costume. Order matters too: `set_register` CLEARS marks (the T1194 form), so the stamp goes AFTER the store. Two mechanisms were proposed and BOTH retracted by measurement rather than by reading: 'the receiver is stamped with the type PARAMETER's name' died when renaming `T` to `Payload` across the whole program (with an assertion that no bare `T` survived) changed nothing — the trace still printed `T`, so the name comes from the stdlib, not the program, and the defect is INSENSITIVE TO THE PARAMETER'S SPELLING. That last fact is the useful constraint: whatever writes the wrong thing at the format site does not read the parameter's name, which weakens — without cancelling — the link to A-TYPARAM's length rule. **T1206 is FIXED (3ae6fd629)** and `04-result-error` is now deterministic and tier-correct — verified by running its binary twice — so the count is 1, and the one that remains is T1210's `16-context-system`. | P0 | T1206 (fixed), T1207, T1210; `vcs/specs/L0-critical/vbc/sum-payload-through-result-is-its-declared-type.vr` | tier parity on all three chapters, asserted on the VALUES; a spec that fails on the unfixed compiler (verified: three addresses, `number=42` correct) |

| A-DATASTRIDE | **User DATA chooses which memory the generated code reads.** A reference to a packed `[Byte; N]` is indexed EIGHT bytes at a time under AOT; the interpreter is right. `arr[1]` answers 66 and `(&arr[..])[1]` answers 0, on the same array in the same program, for all four ways of taking the reference. The width was PREDICTED before the first run and matched to the bit — `[Byte; 8]` filled 1..8 reads `0x0807060504030201` at index 0. **The second half is why this is P0 rather than a wrong number.** The classifier reads the array's OWN FIRST WORD as a `type_id` and switches on it, so three arrays identical except for byte 0 give three different wrong answers: first word `6` (TypeId::U8) -> 0, `7` (U16) -> 0, `99` (no case) -> `0x4D00000063`. The 6/7 arms compute `data = src + OBJECT_HEADER_SIZE` while `src` already points AT the data, so the read lands 24 bytes past the start of a 16-byte array. Byte buffers are what programs fill with INPUT — a length prefix, a protocol tag, a file header — so the value steering the branch is routinely external. **WHY IT STAYED QUIET:** stride 8 is CORRECT for `[Int; N]`, so the only observers are byte buffers, which is exactly what `core/` uses for syscall scratch (`copy_path_nul(path_buf: &mut [Byte; 4096], …)` — the SIGSEGV half of T1192). Carried since at least July as `#48 slice-elemsize` in `core-tests/INVENTORY.md` with no root. **TWO FIXES WERE WRITTEN AND BOTH REVERTED**, and the second failure is the useful one: both rested on an unverified premise (`emit_container_view` classifies this object correctly), disproved by contradiction — the direct read is right and the slice read is wrong on ONE object, so they cannot be consulting one classifier. The element width is a STATIC fact; a third runtime probe is the same move again. **FIXED for the READ side 92482af33** — `RefSlice` carries the declaration's stride as a fifth operand and, on 1/2/4/8, builds the cell without entering the runtime classification at all; the spec passes at both tiers, all seven lines, and a 22-program blast shows moved=0 on both tiers. **TWO GAPS REMAIN AND ARE WHY THIS ROW STAYS.** (a) The hint exists only for a BARE VARIABLE, because `get_typed_array_elem_size` is keyed by NAME — `self.buf[..]` and `xs[i][..]` get 0 and go back to reading the array's first word as a `type_id`, so the content-decides-the-branch behaviour survives for a field or element receiver. Widening the match is not enough; the tracking must become place-based. (b) The WRITE side is untouched: an array PARAMETER is marked `mark_list_register` regardless of element type (`vbc_lowering.rs:3517`), a second road to the same wrong width, and it is what still fails T1192. The sharpest instance of the A-VALUETYPE class (T1190). **2026-09-07 — THREE MORE MEMBERS, and the row's own conclusion holds in all three: the width is a STATIC fact and every runtime probe is the same move again.** (1) T1192, FIXED (1f0631c74): `.len()` on a packed `[Byte; N]` answered 0 at Tier 1 because every probe in `lower_len` reads FORWARD from a pointer that has nothing in front of it — `verum_cbgr_allocate` returns the USER address and its 32-byte `AllocationHeader` sits BEHIND, carrying `{base_offset, total}`, no `type_id` and no length. The interpreter allocates the same array with an OBJECT header carrying a type_id, which is the whole tier disagreement and is now recorded in `ffi-byte-buffer-contract.md` §1b. Answered from the frontend, where `N` is part of the type. **The same fix was tried in the backend first and COULD NOT work**: a per-register fact needs the size operand to be an LLVM constant, and `get_register` emits a `build_load` because alloca mode is unconditional — so `get_zero_extended_constant()` answers None at every such site in every program. Documented at `get_register` itself (3bb3d35ff). (2) T1220, FIXED (cdb69e528): the `NewByteArray` arm never read operand 2, the INIT VALUE, so `[7; 4]` allocated and wrote nothing. `[0; N]` was correct BY ACCIDENT — a fresh allocation already reads zero — which is why it hid in the shape that uses it most, a zeroed scratch buffer. Now filled through `llvm.memset` with the init register as the value operand; verified at both tiers, 7s and all, with the LIST literal form as a measured control that the fill did not disturb the path which already wrote. (3) T1223 (P0): the `NewTypedArray` arm passes the ELEMENT COUNT to `verum_cbgr_allocate` as a BYTE size while its own comment says `size = count * element_size` — a `[Int; 100]` gets 100 bytes for 800, so writes past index 12 leave the allocation. For a BYTE array count == size, which is why 0x30 and 0x34 look like the same code. **A mechanical census bounds the class**: of five emission sites found, `0x33`/`0x37` read every operand — and `0x37` reads its raw stride byte by walking the cursor past three variable-length registers, the exact discipline `0x34` skipped. The right way is one arm over from where it is done wrong, twice. Five sites is a LOWER BOUND, not a closure. **2026-09-08 — A FOURTH MEMBER, T1269 (P0, FIXED), and it retires gap (b) for the LENGTH channel.** A `[T; N]` forgot its length the moment it crossed a function boundary by reference, at Tier 1 and only there: `&[Byte; 12]`, `&mut [Byte; 12]`, `&a`, `&a[..]`, `&mut a`, `&mut a[..]` all answered **0**, a `List`-backed slice answered 12, and a bare `&ai` on a `[Int; 5]` answered **1** — three different wrong answers from one class. WRITES WERE NEVER AFFECTED, which is what made it quiet: a loop to a LITERAL 12 filled the caller's array correctly, so only a loop bounded by `buf.len()` did nothing, and reported success. It cost `core.security.token`: `generate_hex(16)` returned thirty-two zeros at Tier 1 (T1267), the CSPRNG fill being a loop over `buf.len()`, with four callers in security code. THE CONTROL THAT NAMED THE LINK, in one binary before the fix: `f(&a[0..12])` = 12 while `f(&a[..])` = 0 — literal bounds emit no `Len` at all, so the callee side was never at fault and the zero was manufactured at the CALLER and travelled INTO the FatRef as its length. T1192's frontend answer was right and given at ONE of six asking sites; the count is now recorded for every `[T; N]` (including an array PARAMETER, whose N is in its own declaration) and folded at `.len()`, `is_empty()`, the free `len(x)`, `&a[..]`, `a[..]` and a bare `&a`. The row's own conclusion holds a fourth time: the length is a STATIC fact and every runtime probe is the same move again. The one runtime change is the opposite move and is a coherence fix, not a new heuristic — the LIST arm of `lower_len` hand-rolled two of the three shapes `emit_container_view` already arbitrates, and read `@32` unconditionally, which is past a 24-byte cell's allocation. Gap (a) — the tracking is keyed by NAME, so `self.buf[..]` and `xs[i][..]` still get nothing — is UNTOUCHED and is why this row stays. | P1 | T1213, T1192 (fixed), T1190, T1220 (fixed), T1223; `vcs/specs/L0-critical/vbc/byte-array-slice-reads-one-byte.vr` | that spec green at BOTH tiers — it is verified to fail today on six of its seven lines, and `differential` makes the interpreter the oracle, so no expected value in it was invented |

This document names task ids as the trackers for open work, and SIX of
those citations point at a task that has since closed:

    :494  "STILL OPEN, anchors refreshed (T0103)"          T0103 done
    :497  "T0393 (AOT Shared-carrier deref) open"          T0393 done

**A closed task does not close the row.** T0108 was re-measured on
2026-09-07 and its residue reproduces — `ptr_is_aligned` is absent from
the intrinsic registry — so the row is right and the citation is stale.
That is the expected shape here and the reason this list is a note
rather than a set of edits: each one needs the row's own measurement,
not a status lookup.

Only :511 and the `production-readiness.md` T0812 sentence have been
re-measured and annotated. The other five are unverified and are listed
so nobody has to find them twice.

Found by cross-referencing every `T\d{4}` in `docs/` against the task
pool's done/dead sets — the same lens that found five muted guards in
`vcs/specs` (de14a8003) and the nanoid pins in `core-tests`
(541d7347e). THE REFERENCE DOCUMENTATION SITE — a separate repository, not this one — is CLEAN by the same lens: zero pages
call a closed task open.

Layer 5 (83be4c7aa): `&addr_buf[..addr_len as Int]` mis-parsed as `(&addr_buf[..addr_len]) as Int` (cast binds to the whole range) → parenthesize `[..(addr_len as Int)]` at all 11 deserialize sites (a for-loop `0..n as Int` parses correctly — slice-context quirk). | B10 | ~~**`static_assert` asserts NOTHING**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-11 (T0384)** — 546d9dd87. The construct was declared in `infer/env.rs` and implemented in neither tier: 154 calls type-checked and asserted nothing, while anything reaching codegen died on "undefined function". Now folded at compile time — true discharges silently, false is an error carrying the assertion's message, and **unevaluable is also an error** (an assertion the compiler could not check has not been honoured). A failed assertion halts the build in EVERY mode via the new `SkipClass::Fatal`, because codegen's default mode is lenient and would otherwise have dropped the function with a warning — trading one false green for a more convincing one. Accepts one or two arguments: the corpus writes `static_assert(cond, "why")` while the environment declared that form under `static_assert_msg`, which nothing writes. Sequenced AFTER the `@repr(C)` layout fix (A32) so the corpus's assertions are TRUE when enforcement arrives. **Residual, deliberately scoped**: named constants (`SHADOW_SPACE_SIZE == 32`, 2 uses) and `size_of_ptr()` (1 use) do not fold — codegen has no name→value map (`ctx.constants` is a value POOL; `pending_constants` is drained before bodies compile) — so those files report "cannot be evaluated" instead of compiling. Not a regression: they failed with "undefined function" before. The other five declared-but-unimplemented builtins (`static_assert_msg`, `debug_assert_msg`, `writeln`, `watch_channel`, `properties_of`) remain — `properties_of` is the notable one, being the only `.vr`-level evidence for Computational Properties. | P1 | `crates/verum_vbc/src/codegen/expressions.rs` (const_eval + arm), `codegen/error.rs` (`SkipClass::Fatal`) | a deliberately false `static_assert` fails the build in DEFAULT mode; struct_layout.vr's C-layout assertions hold |

(a) **INVENTORY gate** — `no_new_duplicate_module_rows_in_inventory` failed on `sys/mod: 2 rows`: the duplicate the gate exists to prevent was already in the file when it landed. A 2026-08-08 census added a `sys/mod` row believing none existed; one did, spelled `` `sys/mod` (umbrella) ``, and the suffix hid it (differently-spelled-key drift). FIXED here — rows merged, newer measurement governs (39 compile-error / 0 passing, counts zeroed, 2026-05-27 figures kept as history), and `KNOWN_DUPLICATE_ROWS` emptied: all 17 entries were frozen at `1`, which is the NON-duplicate case, so they excused nothing and could never go stale — an inert list that read as an allowance list. (b) **Unit tests (arm)** — exit 143, *"the runner has received a shutdown signal"*, after ~22 min of compile + stdlib bake; no test ever ran. The bake accounts for ~19 of those 22 minutes (a single 17:20→17:40 gap in the log). It is NOT an unconditional cost: measuring the largest inter-line gap in two other jobs of the same run gives 0.7 min and 0.1 min, so those jobs did not bake — i.e. this is a cache MISS on that job's `shared-key: unit-<os>` (rust-cache is configured), and the exposure is that one cold cache on this job exceeds the runner's budget. (Caveat on the measurement: gaps were computed over the first 400 timestamped lines per job.) The standing stdlib-delivery-speed directive makes the bake's cost in CI worth attacking directly, but the fix is cache scope, not "the build is slow". (c) **VCS L0 + L1 (100% gate)** — `RESULT: FAILED (45 failures)`, `L0: 610/656 (93.0%)`. Of those 45, **30 are TIMEOUTS** (28 at ~5000 ms, 2 at 30000 ms) and only **15 are genuine fast failures** (~700-800 ms) — so two-thirds of that gate's red is a time budget, not 45 broken features, and triaging it as 45 defects would be wrong work. | P1 | `crates/verum_vbc/tests/inventory_module_rows_unique.rs`, `core-tests/INVENTORY.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | INVENTORY gate green; (b) and (c) re-measured before any triage |
| A33 | **A diagnostic can lose its source location — silently** (2026-08-11, T0384). Measured: `error<E400>: Type mismatch: expected 'Int', found 'Bool'` prints `--> file.vr:2:18` for a two-line program and prints **no location line at all** for `vcs/specs/L3-extended/ffi/c_interop/struct_layout.vr` (461 lines). Same error code, same renderer, different producer. `TypeError::Mismatch` carries a `span`, so the loss is between construction and the Diagnostic's primary label. `RichRenderer::render_location_header` rendered the header only `if let Some(span) = diagnostic.primary_span()` **with no else**, so "location lost" was indistinguishable from "location not needed" — the reader was left to search the file by hand, which is what made this survive. Renderer now prints `<no source location attached to this diagnostic>` instead: it invents no position, it makes the producer's failure visible and greppable. **RESOLVED 2026-08-11 (cffb33cbb + the W_NOEFFECT leg)** — 20 of the 24 positionless builders now carry a span. Root was not a lost span but an ABSENT one: `DiagnosticBuilder::warning().message(..).build()` with no span call, inside `check_cast`, whose signature takes `span: Span` as a PARAMETER — 15 of the 24 sat in that one function. The rest: 2 in `infer_expr_throw_expr` (`expr.span`), 2 in `check_function_inner` (`func.span`), 1 W_NOEFFECT (`stmt.span`). Measured on the corpus that surfaced it: 4 positionless diagnostics before, 1 after, and the cast warnings now read `struct_layout.vr:380:9`. The type system caught the first attempt — the builder wants `LineColSpan` and the checker carries byte-offset `Span`; `span_to_line_col` was already imported in the same file. **Remaining 4, each with its reason rather than a plan**: `TypeError::Other` (**re-measured 2026-08-11: 336** constructions in verum_types — **and the means to fix them ALREADY EXISTS**, which changes the work entirely. `TypeError` carries three siblings: `Other(Text)` (no code, no span) used **336** times, `OtherWithCode { code, msg }` **6** times, and `OtherWithCodeSpanned { code, msg, span }` — the complete form — **10** times. The best option is used 10 times and the worst 336: not a missing capability, a path-of-least-resistance problem. Recorded from experience, because it is the session's recurring shape landing in my own work: I began adding a FOURTH variant before the compiler's exhaustiveness check pointed me at the existing siblings eighty lines up the same enum. Reverted; three call sites now use `OtherWithCodeSpanned` with `E406`. No new type is needed — the rest is per-site, and **35 of them sit in scopes that already hold a span** — `expr.span`/`stmt.span`/`func.span`, 33 in `infer/expr.rs` alone. Those 35 are a per-site RE-TARGETING onto the existing `TypeError::LanguageLaw { code, message, help, span }` variant, NOT a variant widening; the other 329 have no span in scope and are the campaign-scale part. First one landed: the explicit-type-argument arity error now raises `LanguageLaw` with `E408` plus a help line — **and it exposed the NEXT layer**: the marker is gone and the file is named, but the position renders `1:1` (file start) rather than the call site, because `expr.span` is a DEFAULT at that point. Controlled: a `let x: Int = true;` on line 6 of a blank-padded file reports `6:18`, so spans work in general; the earlier `E100: unbound variable: ln` also reported `1:1`. **Localised further, and the wall is narrower than feared**: an ordinary generic function reports correctly — `ident<Int, Int>(5)` on line 6 of a padded file gives `span_generic.vr:6:13`. The `1:1` case is `bits_of`, declared `public meta fn`, i.e. the META path. The parser does set a real span on a call (`lhs_span.merge(self.stream.make_span(start_pos))` in fast_parser/expr.rs), so it is lost after parsing, and `crates/verum_compiler/src/meta/` contains **99** `Span::default()` / `Span::dummy()` occurrences — synthesised meta nodes carry no origin. So the retarget IS sufficient for ordinary code; only meta-expanded expressions land at 1:1, and fixing those means carrying the call site through meta expansion — `E402` was checked against the registry and is taken by "Send bound not satisfied", and a code is a name, so reusing one would create a second meaning under it) and `RecursionLimit` have NO span FIELD, so localising them means widening the variant across every construction site — campaign-scale, not a follow-up; `W_STDLIB_SHADOW` is raised by `glob_shadow_arbiter(name, provenance)`, which genuinely has no position to give. The one E400 still positionless in the corpus reaches `convert_span` with a span it cannot map; the release backtrace is symbol-stripped, so localising it needs a debug build. Old sizing follows. **Residual, now sized**: `verum_types/src` contains **725** non-test uses of `Span::default()` / `Span::dummy()` / `dummy_span()`, and of the 244 `TypeError::Mismatch` constructions 123 pass `span` by field shorthand (so their quality depends on whatever the enclosing scope bound) — i.e. a diagnostic can be born positionless along hundreds of paths. Do NOT sweep all 725: the renderer marker added here makes the ones that actually reach a user visible, so the targeted procedure is (1) compile the conformance corpus, (2) collect every diagnostic printing `<no source location attached to this diagnostic>`, (3) fix only those producers. The `struct_layout.vr` E400 persists with its `const _: () = { static_assert(..) };` block commented out, so that construct is measured NOT to be the cause. | P1 | `crates/verum_diagnostics/src/rich_renderer.rs` `render_location_header`; producers in `verum_types` | no `<no source location…>` line on any spec in the conformance corpus **RESIDUAL 2026-09-04 (T1073), and it is the OTHER root:** the injectable-context rule (`env.rs`, `convert_type_bounds_to_protocol_bounds`) still prints `--> <no source location attached to this diagnostic>`, and here the span is not absent but LOST — `bound.span` is in scope at the site. `TypeError::Other` becomes `VerumError::Other` in `error_conversions.rs`, and that variant has no field for a code or a span, so both are dropped by the conversion rather than never set. The obvious repair — record it on the diagnostics channel with `push_diagnostic_for`, the way `arity_error` does — was built and REVERTED: it turned E604 into a code + file:line:col + snippet on the negative pole and reddened the POSITIVE one, because `is_declared_context(N) && get_protocol(N).is_none()` is wrong on an early pass and a returned Err is discarded and re-asked after registration while a recording is not. Closing this means giving `VerumError` room for a code and a span, which is a workspace-wide change to the unified error type. |

8696 mounts; 1374 `@intrinsic` occurrences. Full enumerations +
reproducible scripts: audit scratchpad (cat1_dups.txt, cat2_drift.txt,
cat4_unresolved.txt).

| C8 | **The PRELUDE resolves 14 of its 20 math functions to SQLite's SQL builtins** (measured 2026-08-11 through `mount core.prelude.*`): sin cos tan asin acos atan exp log10 log2 sqrt pow abs floor ceil each render `Relaxed` — variant 0 of an unrelated type — instead of a number; log cbrt round min max clamp are correct. The winner is decided by (NAME, ARITY) first-wins: SQLite's `log(v, base_opt)` and `round(v, digits)` take two parameters, so those two names do not collide and math wins. DIRECT mounts are unaffected (`mount core.math.{sqrt}` -> 4.0), so the live surface is the prelude alone, not the 113-collision figure in C-adjacent notes. Bake-side witness: the precompile pass reports `canonicalised 2304 re-export leaves; 2678 left unresolved`, head of list `core.math.E, core.math.INFINITY`. **LARGELY CLOSED 2026-08-12 (T0148, 328fbf309)** — the named form (`mount core.prelude.{sqrt}`) now answers correctly for ALL 20: sin=0 cos=1 tan=0 asin=0 acos=0 atan=0 exp=1 log=0 log10=0 log2=0 sqrt=4 cbrt=2 pow=8 abs=4 floor=2 ceil=3 round=3 min=3 max=7 clamp=5.0. RESIDUAL, and it is an OVERLOAD question rather than a carry bug: the GLOB form (`mount core.prelude.*`) reaches 18 of 20 — `pow` and `abs` still resolve to SQLite because an earlier binding already occupies the name (`VERUM_TRACE_MOUNTGATE` reports `env_occupied=true ambient=false` for both, `env_occupied=false` for the eighteen that were fixed). That occupant is NOT wrong: for `abs` it is the integer-capable binding that 27 files under core-tests/ and vcs/specs/ exercise with integer literals, two of them L0-critical (`assert_eq(abs(-9), 9)`, `assert_eq(abs(-5), 5)`). Forcing the prelude's `abs(x: Float) -> Float{>= 0.0}` to win was tried and REVERTED — it made every one of those calls die on a runtime refinement violation. Serving both needs overload resolution over the bare-name surface, which is a language decision, not a resolution-priority tweak. ROOT (corrected 2026-08-11, two instruments): name resolution is NOT the blocker. `VERUM_TRACE_TASK20` shows verum_types resolving the prelude leaf transitively to `core.math.elementary.sqrt` with `functions_has=true`; `VERUM_TRACE_CALLS` shows the interpreter then calling `core.database.sqlite.native.builtins.math_fns.sqrt` (func_id 13848). The type checker's resolved callee is DISCARDED by VBC codegen's bare-name call binding — same two-resolvers shape as T0718's `min`. An earlier reading of this entry blamed the bake-vs-loader split (T0691); that was wrong and is retracted. | P1 | `scripts/ci/check_barename_collisions.py --scope prelude` (frozen at 26); T0538 / T0691 journals | `mount core.prelude.*` answers a number for all 20; the prelude scope drops below 26 in the commit that earns it |
| C9 | **Three math CONTRACTS share one flat bare-name space** — measured 2026-08-11: of 97 public (name, arity) pairs across core/math's scalar files, 31 are declared more than once; 18 of them exist in ALL THREE of `elementary.vr` (Layer 1, high precision), `libm.vr` (Layer 0, pure-Verum zero-libc) and `ieee754_deterministic.vr` (Layer 0, correctly-rounded, selected by `@deterministic_fp`): acos asin atan atan2 cbrt cos cosh exp expm1 hypot log10 log2 pow sin sinh sqrt tan tanh. **DO NOT 'DEDUPLICATE' THESE** — they are three deliberate contracts with different guarantees, and collapsing them would silently change rounding behaviour under `@deterministic_fp`. The defect is that a caller cannot SAY which contract it wants through a bare name, so resolution order picks one invisibly; the language already has the selector (`@deterministic_fp`) and the flat name space undermines it. Latent, not a measured live misdispatch inside core.math — all three answer 4.0 for `sqrt(16.0)`. | P2 | `check_barename_collisions.py --typed` (297 frozen); core/math/{elementary,libm,ieee754_deterministic}.vr headers | a caller can select a contract explicitly; the 18 shared names stop being resolvable by order alone |

| C11 | **User code cannot construct a stdlib RECORD against the shipped archive.** A/B on one file, differing only by `VERUM_STDLIB_PATH`: `RsaPublicKey { n, e }` and `TableStats { ... }` fail from the embedded archive with `E400: field 'n' ... expected 'Unit'`, and compile from source; the generic `Edge { src, tgt, label }` fails differently — `expected 'L'`, the type parameter unsubstituted — and also works from source. So concrete records lose field types to `Unit`, generic ones keep them uninstantiated. Narrowed to three candidate sites: `type_descriptor_to_type` drops `Record { .. }` fields in BOTH branches; the field map is built elsewhere (`infer/core.rs` ~1415) and guards an empty `f.ty` with a fresh var; `parse_descriptor_type_string` returns `Type::Unit` for both `""` and `"()"`. **FIXED 2026-08-13 (e3da43a8d)** and it was none of the three: the archive holds SEVERAL descriptors per type name — the declaring module's, with real field types, and one per CONSUMING module, whose fields all carry `FieldDescriptor::default()` (`type_ref = Concrete(TypeId::UNIT)`). Both are `Record`, the collision policy ranked by KIND alone, and sorted-walk order picked the winner. Localised by dumping the RAW archive at test time after three code-read hypotheses failed. Gate: `stdlib_record_field_types_survive_the_archive`; catalogued as §58. **RESIDUAL ALSO FIXED (9210b6e89)**: generic records failed with the type parameter unsubstituted (`Edge { src, tgt, label }` -> `expected 'L'`) for an unrelated reason — `__type_params_<name>`, the key the record-literal checker substitutes through, was registered INSIDE `ensure_stdlib_type_loaded`'s "type not yet loaded" guard, so a type already in ctx short-circuited the helper and its params were never written. The type being loaded and its parameters being loaded are two facts; one guard cannot stand for both. **C11 CLOSED**: both concrete and generic stdlib records now construct from user code against the shipped archive. | P0 | T0724 journal with the three reproductions | archive and source agree, and a stdlib record can be constructed from user code against the shipped archive |
| C12 | **A `meta` (compile-time value) parameter is not delivered into the body.** Nine stdlib functions ship as panic stubs for this one gap — `MambaBlock.new/.forward/.selective_scan`, `StateSpaceKernel.discretize/.hippo_init`, `S4Layer.dplr_init`, `Expert.new`, `MeshTopology.new`, `ActorMesh.spawn`, plus `ClosedSubspaceLattice.top/.bottom` and `EpistemicHilbertSpace.canonical` — each with `undefined variable: N` / `H` / `k` / `DIMS`. TWO SYMPTOMS, and the second is worse: in a minimal case the read is SILENT. `implement<N: meta USize> Buf<N> { fn cap() -> Int { N as Int } }` then `Buf<4>.cap()` prints `()`, not 4 — a Unit with no diagnostic. The declarations parse and typecheck fine, refinement included (`N: meta USize{N > 0}`); what is missing is the VALUE reaching the body, and presumably monomorphisation over it. | P1 | T0726 journal with the minimal reproduction | the minimal case prints 4, and the nine functions leave the bake's skip list |
| C13 | **A check that silently stopped applying below a directory depth.** `E600: circular constant dependency` never fired when the file sat two or more directories under the cog manifest — and the spec covering it read GREEN for four levels. A constant is keyed by its FULL module path while the imported-constant edge was built from the FIRST SEGMENT of that path; the two agree only when the path has one segment, i.e. only beside the manifest. An earlier fix (T0587) had replaced a hardcoded `cog.` with the first segment — repairing the case in front of it and leaving the general one broken. **FIXED 2026-08-14 (33e00587a)**: the edge uses the ENCLOSING path, which is what "sibling module under the same parent" means, and reduces to the old behaviour at depth 1. Found by bisecting on the file's SURROUNDINGS with byte-identical copies, then probing both sides of the comparison the detector performs. | P1 | T0727 journal with the bisection table | verified at depths 1, 2, 3 and in place |

Build scripts run under `check`, so an IDE analyzer re-bakes the stdlib on every touch of any `core/*.vr`, unasked and continuously. Measured chain: `claude(13d) → rust-analyzer(2d16h) → cargo check → build_script_build → cargo + 6 rustc (~9 GB)`. Cost the same day: `Pages free` 17 GB → 3.2 GB, swap 21.4 of 22.5 GB used, and two consecutive release builds killed (exit 144). **CORRECTED 2026-09-04, and most of the original framing was wrong.** The bake is NOT careless: `build.rs:391` compares a blake3 over the curated `core/` list against a stored checksum and skips entirely when content matches — it ran repeatedly that day because `core/net/tcp.vr` was genuinely modified. And the opt-out this row asked someone to write ALREADY EXISTED at `:350` (`VERUM_NO_AUTO_PRECOMPILE`, beside `DOCS_RS`), undocumented anywhere until `668ced6c9`. What survives is narrower and is the real cost: the archive is PER-TARGET-ROOT (`:339-341`) while this repo mandates session-private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`s, so N sessions editing `core/` pay N full bakes for one change and nothing shares the result; and a build killed under pressure loses its in-flight bake, because the checksum is written last (measured: a completed ~60-minute bake discarded with its cargo and immediately re-run). | P1 | T1140 | opt-out env var honoured by build.rs and documented next to the other `VERUM_*` switches; measured: touching one `core/` file then `cargo check` spawns no `verum_stdlib_precompiler` |
